## Thai Boxing

Path: `MUSICIANS/D/Dunn_David/Thai_Boxing.sid`

Author: David Dunn · Release/year: 1986 Anco Software

PSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `c40fa5b93449b8986ad5353270c04076`; SHA-256: `ee25fdd81985b11f27154e1e30bf4896dfce0bd6d30fb27d0babfa33e7339a42`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 1.131e-1; 472 | ΔRMS 1.901e-3; writes 472; prefix 472; drift 13; derived | identical PCM; writes 472; prefix 472; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 1.901e-3; writes 472; prefix 472; drift 13 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/D/Dunn_David/
> COMMENT: Julie (née David) Dunn's own comments are denoted (JD).
>
> COMMENT: "It was an original. It partly came from the electronic percussion
>          used on the pop tracks of the day, like Ring My Bell, and I thought:
>          'I can do that'. I re-wrote the whole loader/driver program to make it
>          work. The tune was easy, it had been buzzing around in my head for
>          ages. Thai Boxing was probably the beginning of the end for me: Roger
>          Gamon had since died of a brain tumour, Ocean had enlisted their own
>          in-house programmer, and as the sole contracted person there, I felt I
>          was becoming disinterested." (JD)

> COMMENT: "Made with Megastyle's editor. I had Quit Cosine, and then got word

>          intro/demo-tunes. I got their editor but they never used ANY of my
>          music. Their player was based on JB/MON's Stormlord tunes. I decided
>          to join my local friends in the Norway/Finland/Germany-based group
>          Antimon instead. Most of my music had been used by them anyway. This
>          is like most of my tunes in in C-minor and rather sad sounding. I put
>          a lot of work into this one (and not quitting early like usually do).
>          I used this tune in a demopart with an atom-plotter (Antimon is an
>          atom ya know). Editor=Megastyle-editor" (ELA)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/E/ELA/
> COMMENT: Comments by ELA himself are denoted (ELA). ELA is short for

>
> COMMENT: "One of the reasons I got to join The Suppliers (who were quite big
